========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for fitting me in. I, um... I hope I'm not wasting your time with this, because I know it's probably not a huge deal, but... well, I guess what brings me in is that I keep getting stuck on this sagging, uneven look around my jaw. It's mostly just on one side of my face. 

I really started fixating on it after seeing myself in a bunch of recent family photos, and now I just can't seem to unsee it. I know it probably sounds a bit vain or silly for a guy my age to worry about... and my wife, and even my clients at the firm, they all swear they barely notice anything at all. But it's just been bothering me a lot more than it probably should. Does that make sense? I really hope that's the sort of thing it's okay to talk about here.

Well, um... I guess I do check it a little bit. I mean, not constantly, but... right before I meet with a client, I might just pop into the bathroom real quick to look in the mirror. And maybe I'll just, you know, glance at my phone camera a couple of times. It's just to make sure the lighting isn't making it look worse than it is. Sometimes I'll adjust my collar a bit, or just try to make sure I'm sitting at an angle where the shadow isn't so obvious. It's just a quick check, really, just to be sure I look presentable. That's pretty normal, right? Wanting to look professional?

And I guess... well, I do catch myself looking at other people sometimes. Like, if I'm at an event or even just looking at some of the younger guys at the firm. I'll kind of, um, compare my jaw to theirs. I know I shouldn't, and I really try to focus on what they're saying, but my mind just sort of slips into doing it. I hope that doesn't make me sound too superficial. I'm really just trying to figure out if it's as noticeable as I think it is.

Well, um... I wouldn't say it's severe distress or anything like that. I'm definitely not having panic attacks or, you know, completely falling apart over it. It's more just... a mild annoyance, I guess? Like a low-level hum of anxiety in the background.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... that's a really good question. I mean, I definitely see it when I look in the mirror. It looks... off to me. But, I don't know, maybe I'm just being my own worst critic? I certainly don't want you to think I'm, like, completely out of touch with reality or anything. 

I think a lot of it is just... well, in my line of work, projecting confidence is so important. When you're advising people on their finances, they need to see someone who looks put-together and capable. So I guess I just worry that if my jaw looks weak or uneven, clients might subconsciously judge me for it, or maybe take me less seriously. 

So it's probably a bit of both, but mostly the worry about how it comes across to other people. That makes sense, right? Wanting to make a good, solid impression professionally? I'm sure most people in business worry about that sort of thing... don't they?

I still get through my workday, you know? I'm still doing my job. But... I guess I have been a little slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. I just end up spending a bit too much time checking the mirror, trying to make sure the lighting isn't catching it too badly. \n\nAnd, honestly, I might be a little less present during some of those appointments. My mind just drifts to wondering if they're looking at my jaw. I've also, um... I've skipped a few local art openings recently with my wife. I just really didn't want to be seen, or, you know, end up in the background of someone's photos. I know that sounds a bit foolish, letting something so small get in the way. I'm sorry, I hope I'm not overreacting by bringing this up... is it normal for something like this to be so distracting?\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing anything repeatedly, like checking your appearance or trying to fix your jaw, in response to this concern?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do check it a little bit. I mean, not constantly, but... right before I meet with a client, I might just pop into the bathroom real quick to look in the mirror. And maybe I'll just, you know, glance at my phone camera a couple of times. It's just to make sure the lighting isn't making it look worse than it is. Sometimes I'll adjust my collar a bit, or just try to make sure I'm sitting at an angle where the shadow isn't so obvious. It's just a quick check, really, just to be sure I look presentable. That's pretty normal, right? Wanting to look professional?\n\nAnd I guess... well, I do catch myself looking at other people sometimes. Like, if I'm at an event or even just looking at some of the younger guys at the firm. I'll kind of, um, compare my jaw to theirs. I know I shouldn't, and I really try to focus on what they're saying, but my mind just sort of slips into doing it. I hope that doesn't make me sound too superficial. I'm really just trying to figure out if it's as noticeable as I think it is.\n\nDoctor: How much distress or anxiety would you say this concern about your jaw is causing you?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say it's severe distress or anything like that. I'm definitely not having panic attacks or, you know, completely falling apart over it. It's more just... a mild annoyance, I guess? Like a low-level hum of anxiety in the background. \n\nIt's just that it bothers me when I think about it too much, but I can usually push it aside. It's not like I'm sitting around crying about it. I mean, it's just a jaw, right? It's silly to get too worked up over it. I just... I wish I didn't think about it as much as I do. But it's really not that bad, honestly. I'm sure you see people who are dealing with much, much worse. I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time with something so minor. Does that make sense? I just want to be honest without making it sound like a bigger deal than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these checks or adjustments at specific times or in a specific way?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no, definitely not. I don't have, like, a strict routine or anything like that. It's not like I *have* to do it at a certain time of day or in a specific order. I don't want you to think I'm... you know, doing some kind of strange ritual. \n\nIt's really just when the thought pops up, or right before a meeting like I mentioned. Just a quick glance. I don't have any kind of rigid rules for it. That would probably be a bit much, wouldn't it? I just try to keep it casual... just a normal kind of grooming check, basically. I hope that's what most guys do? I definitely don't have a whole system for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your jaw looks the way you think it does, or is it mostly that you're worried about what others might think of it?\n\nPatient: Well, um... that's a really good question. I mean, I definitely see it when I look in the mirror. It looks... off to me. But, I don't know, maybe I'm just being my own worst critic? I certainly don't want you to think I'm, like, completely out of touch with reality or anything. \n\nI think a lot of it is just... well, in my line of work, projecting confidence is so important. When you're advising people on their finances, they need to see someone who looks put-together and capable. So I guess I just worry that if my jaw looks weak or uneven, clients might subconsciously judge me for it, or maybe take me less seriously. \n\nSo it's probably a bit of both, but mostly the worry about how it comes across to other people. That makes sense, right? Wanting to make a good, solid impression professionally? I'm sure most people in business worry about that sort of thing... don't they?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Oh, um... well, to be completely honest, no one has ever actually said anything to me about it. So I don't have any, like, proof that they're looking at it. I guess... it's probably just me worrying about the possibility, like you said. 

I definitely don't walk around thinking everyone is staring at me—I don't want you to think I'm paranoid or anything like that! It's really just that little nagging thought in the back of my mind, especially when I'm sitting across the desk from a new client. But since nobody has ever brought it up or stared, I suppose it's mostly just my own internal worry. 

That's a pretty normal distinction to make, right? Just being a little overly self-conscious rather than actually having people point it out? I hope that sounds reasonable. I just try to be self-aware, I guess.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, wow, um... that's an interesting way to think about it. I guess if I were literally the only person on earth, or if I lived on a deserted island or something... no, I probably wouldn't worry about it nearly as much. 

I mean, I'd still see it in the mirror, obviously, but without the pressure of having to look sharp for clients or... or just wanting to make a good impression in general, it wouldn't feel like such a big deal. 

So much of it is tied to just wanting to present myself well, you know? Being a professional. If nobody was around to see me, I think I'd just let it go. That makes sense, doesn't it? That the social and professional aspect is what really drives the concern? I think most guys in my position would feel the same way if you took the social pressure away.

I certainly don't want you to think I'm, like, completely out of touch with reality or anything. \n\nI think a lot of it is just... well, in my line of work, projecting confidence is so important. When you're advising people on their finances, they need to see someone who looks put-together and capable. I mean, I try to look presentable, obviously—you know, wear a nice suit, keep my hair neat for the clients. But I'm definitely not sitting around worrying about my weight or... or my nose or anything else. I'm pretty much just a regular guy in that department. \n\nI don't want you to think I'm incredibly vain or, like, obsessed with my looks overall. It's really just this one specific thing that kind of... catches my attention. That's fairly normal, right? To just have one little feature that bugs you, but otherwise you're fine with how you look? I hope I'm making sense. I definitely don't have a whole list of things I'm unhappy with or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others actually notice your jaw or is it just that you're worried about the possibility of them noticing it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, to be completely honest, no one has ever actually said anything to me about it. So I don't have any, like, proof that they're looking at it. I guess... it's probably just me worrying about the possibility, like you said. \n\nI definitely don't walk around thinking everyone is staring at me—I don't want you to think I'm paranoid or anything like that! It's really just that little nagging thought in the back of my mind, especially when I'm sitting across the desk from a new client. But since nobody has ever brought it up or stared, I suppose it's mostly just my own internal worry. \n\nThat's a pretty normal distinction to make, right? Just being a little overly self-conscious rather than actually having people point it out? I hope that sounds reasonable. I just try to be self-aware, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be concerned about your jaw if you were the only person who ever saw it?\n\nPatient: Oh, wow, um... that's an interesting way to think about it. I guess if I were literally the only person on earth, or if I lived on a deserted island or something... no, I probably wouldn't worry about it nearly as much. \n\nI mean, I'd still see it in the mirror, obviously, but without the pressure of having to look sharp for clients or... or just wanting to make a good impression in general, it wouldn't feel like such a big deal. \n\nSo much of it is tied to just wanting to present myself well, you know? Being a professional. If nobody was around to see me, I think I'd just let it go. That makes sense, doesn't it? That the social and professional aspect is what really drives the concern? I think most guys in my position would feel the same way if you took the social pressure away.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Oh, um... well, I guess it's a little bit of both, but... mostly it's about what others might think. I mean, I see it, and I don't love it, but if I knew for a fact that absolutely no one else cared or judged me for it, it wouldn't bother me nearly as much.

Like, if I'm at an event or even just looking at some of the younger guys at the firm. I'll kind of, um, compare my jaw to theirs. I know I shouldn't, and I really try to focus on what they're saying, but my mind just sort of slips into doing it. I hope that doesn't make me sound too superficial. I'm really just trying to figure out if it's as noticeable as I think it is.\n\nDoctor: How much distress or anxiety would you say this concern about your jaw is causing you?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say it's severe distress or anything like that. I'm definitely not having panic attacks or, you know, completely falling apart over it. It's more just... a mild annoyance, I guess? Like a low-level hum of anxiety in the background. \n\nIt's just that it bothers me when I think about it too much, but I can usually push it aside. It's not like I'm sitting around crying about it. I mean, it's just a jaw, right? It's silly to get too worked up over it. I just... I wish I didn't think about it as much as I do. But it's really not that bad, honestly. I'm sure you see people who are dealing with much, much worse. I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time with something so minor. Does that make sense? I just want to be honest without making it sound like a bigger deal than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these checks or adjustments at specific times or in a specific way?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no, definitely not. I don't have, like, a strict routine or anything like that. It's not like I *have* to do it at a certain time of day or in a specific order. I don't want you to think I'm... you know, doing some kind of strange ritual. \n\nIt's really just when the thought pops up, or right before a meeting like I mentioned. Just a quick glance. I don't have any kind of rigid rules for it. That would probably be a bit much, wouldn't it? I just try to keep it casual... just a normal kind of grooming check, basically. I hope that's what most guys do? I definitely don't have a whole system for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your jaw looks the way you think it does, or is it mostly that you're worried about what others might think of it?\n\nPatient: Well, um... that's a really good question. I mean, I definitely see it when I look in the mirror. It looks... off to me. But, I don't know, maybe I'm just being my own worst critic? I certainly don't want you to think I'm, like, completely out of touch with reality or anything. \n\nI think a lot of it is just... well, in my line of work, projecting confidence is so important. When you're advising people on their finances, they need to see someone who looks put-together and capable. So I guess I just worry that if my jaw looks weak or uneven, clients might subconsciously judge me for it, or maybe take me less seriously. \n\nSo it's probably a bit of both, but mostly the worry about how it comes across to other people. That makes sense, right? Wanting to make a good, solid impression professionally? I'm sure most people in business worry about that sort of thing... don't they?\n\nDoctor: Is your concern primarily about this specific issue with your jaw, or do you also worry about your overall appearance?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, it's really just the jaw. I mean, I try to look presentable, obviously—you know, wear a nice suit, keep my hair neat for the clients. But I'm definitely not sitting around worrying about my weight or... or my nose or anything else. I'm pretty much just a regular guy in that department. \n\nI don't want you to think I'm incredibly vain or, like, obsessed with my looks overall. It's really just this one specific thing that kind of... catches my attention. That's fairly normal, right? To just have one little feature that bugs you, but otherwise you're fine with how you look? I hope I'm making sense. I definitely don't have a whole list of things I'm unhappy with or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others actually notice your jaw or is it just that you're worried about the possibility of them noticing it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, to be completely honest, no one has ever actually said anything to me about it. So I don't have any, like, proof that they're looking at it. I guess... it's probably just me worrying about the possibility, like you said. \n\nI definitely don't walk around thinking everyone is staring at me—I don't want you to think I'm paranoid or anything like that! It's really just that little nagging thought in the back of my mind, especially when I'm sitting across the desk from a new client. But since nobody has ever brought it up or stared, I suppose it's mostly just my own internal worry. \n\nThat's a pretty normal distinction to make, right? Just being a little overly self-conscious rather than actually having people point it out? I hope that sounds reasonable. I just try to be self-aware, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be concerned about your jaw if you were the only person who ever saw it?\n\nPatient: Oh, wow, um... that's an interesting way to think about it. I guess if I were literally the only person on earth, or if I lived on a deserted island or something... no, I probably wouldn't worry about it nearly as much. \n\nI mean, I'd still see it in the mirror, obviously, but without the pressure of having to look sharp for clients or... or just wanting to make a good impression in general, it wouldn't feel like such a big deal. \n\nSo much of it is tied to just wanting to present myself well, you know? Being a professional. If nobody was around to see me, I think I'd just let it go. That makes sense, doesn't it? That the social and professional aspect is what really drives the concern? I think most guys in my position would feel the same way if you took the social pressure away.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your concern about your jaw is more about the way it actually looks or about what others might think of you because of it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I guess it's a little bit of both, but... mostly it's about what others might think. I mean, I see it, and I don't love it, but if I knew for a fact that absolutely no one else cared or judged me for it, it wouldn't bother me nearly as much. \n\nIt's just... in my line of work, people are trusting you with their life savings, you know? You want to look like someone who has everything together. So I just worry that if my jaw looks... off, or weak, or whatever, they might subconsciously think I'm not capable or confident. \n\nI'm not saying I'm terrified of people judging me or anything extreme like that! I just want to project competence. That's a pretty normal thing to care about when you're dealing with clients, right? I just try to be mindful of how I'm coming across. I hope that doesn't make me sound too insecure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Oh, um... "significant distress" sounds a bit intense. I wouldn't say it's like, ruining my life or anything like that. I still get through all my work and manage my accounts just fine.

Oh, um... well, if I knew for absolute certain that everyone else thought it looked totally fine? I guess... I guess I probably wouldn't worry about it.

I don't want you to think I'm... you know, doing some kind of strange ritual. \n\nIt's really just when the thought pops up, or right before a meeting like I mentioned. Just a quick glance. I don't have any kind of rigid rules for it. That would probably be a bit much, wouldn't it? I just try to keep it casual... just a normal kind of grooming check, basically. I hope that's what most guys do? I definitely don't have a whole system for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your jaw looks the way you think it does, or is it mostly that you're worried about what others might think of it?\n\nPatient: Well, um... that's a really good question. I mean, I definitely see it when I look in the mirror. It looks... off to me. But, I don't know, maybe I'm just being my own worst critic? I certainly don't want you to think I'm, like, completely out of touch with reality or anything. \n\nI think a lot of it is just... well, in my line of work, projecting confidence is so important. When you're advising people on their finances, they need to see someone who looks put-together and capable. So I guess I just worry that if my jaw looks weak or uneven, clients might subconsciously judge me for it, or maybe take me less seriously. \n\nSo it's probably a bit of both, but mostly the worry about how it comes across to other people. That makes sense, right? Wanting to make a good, solid impression professionally? I'm sure most people in business worry about that sort of thing... don't they?\n\nDoctor: Is your concern primarily about this specific issue with your jaw, or do you also worry about your overall appearance?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, it's really just the jaw. I'm pretty much just a regular guy in that department. \n\nI don't want you to think I'm incredibly vain or, like, obsessed with my looks overall. I hope I'm making sense. I definitely don't have a whole list of things I'm unhappy with or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others actually notice your jaw or is it just that you're worried about the possibility of them noticing it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, to be completely honest, no one has ever actually said anything to me about it. I guess I have been a little slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. Just taking a bit more time in the mirror, making sure the angle is right. I'm still functioning perfectly well overall. That doesn't sound too bad, does it? I just like to be careful about my image.\n\nDoctor: If you were certain that your jaw looked perfectly normal to everyone else, would you still be concerned about its appearance?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, if I knew for absolute certain that everyone else thought it looked totally fine? I guess... I guess I probably wouldn't worry about it. \n\nI mean, if I'm being honest, I'd probably still look in the mirror and think it could be better, you know? But if I was guaranteed that no one else was noticing anything wrong, I think I could let it go. It's really just about making sure I'm putting my best foot forward with people. \n\nIf I knew they weren't seeing what I'm seeing, then there'd be no reason to stress over it, right? Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... yeah, I think I pretty much just said that, didn't I? I hope I'm being clear enough.

Oh, um... well, I guess I'm not entirely sure how to separate those two things. I mean, I definitely think it looks a bit uneven, you know? Like I said, I can see it in the mirror.

I know it probably sounds a bit vain or silly for a guy my age to worry about... and my wife, and even my clients at the firm, they all swear they barely notice anything at all. I really hope that's the sort of thing it's okay to talk about here.\n\nDoctor: Is this concern about your jaw affecting your daily activities or relationships in any way?\n\nPatient: Well, um, I wouldn't say it's ruining my life or anything dramatic like that. I still get through my workday, you know? I'm still doing my job. But... I guess I have been a little slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. I just end up spending a bit too much time checking the mirror, trying to make sure the lighting isn't catching it too badly. \n\nAnd, honestly, I might be a little less present during some of those appointments. My mind just drifts to wondering if they're looking at my jaw. I've also, um... I've skipped a few local art openings recently with my wife. Sometimes I'll adjust my collar a bit, or just try to make sure I'm sitting at an angle where the shadow isn't so obvious. It's just a quick check, really, just to be sure I look presentable. That's pretty normal, right? Wanting to look professional?\n\nAnd I guess... well, I do catch myself looking at other people sometimes. Like, if I'm at an event or even just looking at some of the younger guys at the firm. I'll kind of, um, compare my jaw to theirs. I know I shouldn't, and I really try to focus on what they're saying, but my mind just sort of slips into doing it. I hope that doesn't make me sound too superficial. I'm really just trying to figure out if it's as noticeable as I think it is.\n\nDoctor: How much distress or anxiety would you say this concern about your jaw is causing you?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say it's severe distress or anything like that. I'm definitely not having panic attacks or, you know, completely falling apart over it. It's more just... a mild annoyance, I guess? Like a low-level hum of anxiety in the background. \n\nIt's just that it bothers me when I think about it too much, but I can usually push it aside. It's not like I'm sitting around crying about it. I mean, it's just a jaw, right? It's silly to get too worked up over it. I just... I wish I didn't think about it as much as I do. But it's really not that bad, honestly. I'm sure you see people who are dealing with much, much worse. I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time with something so minor. Does that make sense? I just want to be honest without making it sound like a bigger deal than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these checks or adjustments at specific times or in a specific way?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no, definitely not. I don't have any kind of rigid rules for it. That would probably be a bit much, wouldn't it? I just try to keep it casual... just a normal kind of grooming check, basically. I hope that's what most guys do? I definitely don't have a whole system for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your jaw looks the way you think it does, or is it mostly that you're worried about what others might think of it?\n\nPatient: Well, um... that's a really good question. I mean, I definitely see it when I look in the mirror. It looks... off to me. But, I don't know, maybe I'm just being my own worst critic? I certainly don't want you to think I'm, like, completely out of touch with reality or anything. \n\nI think a lot of it is just... well, in my line of work, projecting confidence is so important. When you're advising people on their finances, they need to see someone who looks put-together and capable. So I guess I just worry that if my jaw looks weak or uneven, clients might subconsciously judge me for it, or maybe take me less seriously. \n\nSo it's probably a bit of both, but mostly the worry about how it comes across to other people. That makes sense, right? Wanting to make a good, solid impression professionally? I'm sure most people in business worry about that sort of thing... don't they?\n\nDoctor: Is your concern primarily about this specific issue with your jaw, or do you also worry about your overall appearance?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, it's really just the jaw. I mean, I try to look presentable, obviously—you know, wear a nice suit, keep my hair neat for the clients. But I'm definitely not sitting around worrying about my weight or... or my nose or anything else. I'm pretty much just a regular guy in that department. \n\nI don't want you to think I'm incredibly vain or, like, obsessed with my looks overall. It's really just this one specific thing that kind of... catches my attention. That's fairly normal, right? To just have one little feature that bugs you, but otherwise you're fine with how you look? I hope I'm making sense. I definitely don't have a whole list of things I'm unhappy with or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others actually notice your jaw or is it just that you're worried about the possibility of them noticing it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, to be completely honest, no one has ever actually said anything to me about it. So I don't have any, like, proof that they're looking at it. I guess... it's probably just me worrying about the possibility, like you said. \n\nI definitely don't walk around thinking everyone is staring at me—I don't want you to think I'm paranoid or anything like that! It's really just that little nagging thought in the back of my mind, especially when I'm sitting across the desk from a new client. But since nobody has ever brought it up or stared, I suppose it's mostly just my own internal worry. \n\nThat's a pretty normal distinction to make, right? Just being a little overly self-conscious rather than actually having people point it out? I hope that sounds reasonable. I just try to be self-aware, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be concerned about your jaw if you were the only person who ever saw it?\n\nPatient: Oh, wow, um... that's an interesting way to think about it. I guess if I were literally the only person on earth, or if I lived on a deserted island or something... no, I probably wouldn't worry about it nearly as much. \n\nI mean, I'd still see it in the mirror, obviously, but without the pressure of having to look sharp for clients or... or just wanting to make a good impression in general, it wouldn't feel like such a big deal. \n\nSo much of it is tied to just wanting to present myself well, you know? Being a professional. If nobody was around to see me, I think I'd just let it go. That makes sense, doesn't it? That the social and professional aspect is what really drives the concern? I think most guys in my position would feel the same way if you took the social pressure away.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your concern about your jaw is more about the way it actually looks or about what others might think of you because of it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I guess it's a little bit of both, but... mostly it's about what others might think. I mean, I see it, and I don't love it, but if I knew for a fact that absolutely no one else cared or judged me for it, it wouldn't bother me nearly as much. \n\nIt's just... in my line of work, people are trusting you with their life savings, you know? You want to look like someone who has everything together. I mean, it's just a jaw, right? It's silly to get too worked up over it. I just... I wish I didn't think about it as much as I do. But it's really not that bad, honestly. I'm sure you see people who are dealing with much, much worse. I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time with something so minor. Does that make sense? I just want to be honest without making it sound like a bigger deal than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these checks or adjustments at specific times or in a specific way?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no, definitely not. I don't have, like, a strict routine or anything like that. It's not like I *have* to do it at a certain time of day or in a specific order. I don't want you to think I'm... you know, doing some kind of strange ritual. \n\nIt's really just when the thought pops up, or right before a meeting like I mentioned. Just a quick glance. I don't have any kind of rigid rules for it. That would probably be a bit much, wouldn't it? I just try to keep it casual... just a normal kind of grooming check, basically. I hope that's what most guys do? I definitely don't have a whole system for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your jaw looks the way you think it does, or is it mostly that you're worried about what others might think of it?\n\nPatient: Well, um... that's a really good question. I mean, I definitely see it when I look in the mirror. It looks... off to me. But, I don't know, maybe I'm just being my own worst critic? I certainly don't want you to think I'm, like, completely out of touch with reality or anything. \n\nI think a lot of it is just... well, in my line of work, projecting confidence is so important. When you're advising people on their finances, they need to see someone who looks put-together and capable. So I guess I just worry that if my jaw looks weak or uneven, clients might subconsciously judge me for it, or maybe take me less seriously. \n\nSo it's probably a bit of both, but mostly the worry about how it comes across to other people. That makes sense, right? Wanting to make a good, solid impression professionally? I'm sure most people in business worry about that sort of thing... don't they?\n\nDoctor: Is your concern primarily about this specific issue with your jaw, or do you also worry about your overall appearance?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, it's really just the jaw. I mean, I try to look presentable, obviously—you know, wear a nice suit, keep my hair neat for the clients. But I'm definitely not sitting around worrying about my weight or... or my nose or anything else. I'm pretty much just a regular guy in that department. \n\nI don't want you to think I'm incredibly vain or, like, obsessed with my looks overall. It's really just this one specific thing that kind of... catches my attention. That's fairly normal, right? To just have one little feature that bugs you, but otherwise you're fine with how you look? I hope I'm making sense. I definitely don't have a whole list of things I'm unhappy with or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others actually notice your jaw or is it just that you're worried about the possibility of them noticing it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, to be completely honest, no one has ever actually said anything to me about it. So I don't have any, like, proof that they're looking at it. I guess... it's probably just me worrying about the possibility, like you said. \n\nI definitely don't walk around thinking everyone is staring at me—I don't want you to think I'm paranoid or anything like that! It's really just that little nagging thought in the back of my mind, especially when I'm sitting across the desk from a new client. But since nobody has ever brought it up or stared, I suppose it's mostly just my own internal worry. \n\nThat's a pretty normal distinction to make, right? Just being a little overly self-conscious rather than actually having people point it out? I hope that sounds reasonable. I just try to be self-aware, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be concerned about your jaw if you were the only person who ever saw it?\n\nPatient: Oh, wow, um... that's an interesting way to think about it. I guess if I were literally the only person on earth, or if I lived on a deserted island or something... no, I probably wouldn't worry about it nearly as much. \n\nI mean, I'd still see it in the mirror, obviously, but without the pressure of having to look sharp for clients or... or just wanting to make a good impression in general, it wouldn't feel like such a big deal. \n\nSo much of it is tied to just wanting to present myself well, you know? Being a professional. If nobody was around to see me, I think I'd just let it go. That makes sense, doesn't it? That the social and professional aspect is what really drives the concern? I think most guys in my position would feel the same way if you took the social pressure away.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your concern about your jaw is more about the way it actually looks or about what others might think of you because of it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I guess it's a little bit of both, but... mostly it's about what others might think. I mean, I see it, and I don't love it, but if I knew for a fact that absolutely no one else cared or judged me for it, it wouldn't bother me nearly as much. \n\nIt's just... in my line of work, people are trusting you with their life savings, you know? You want to look like someone who has everything together. So I just worry that if my jaw looks... off, or weak, or whatever, they might subconsciously think I'm not capable or confident. \n\nI'm not saying I'm terrified of people judging me or anything extreme like that! I just want to project competence. That's a pretty normal thing to care about when you're dealing with clients, right? I just try to be mindful of how I'm coming across. I hope that doesn't make me sound too insecure.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your concern about your jaw is causing you significant distress or impairment in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... \"significant distress\" sounds a bit intense. I wouldn't say it's like, ruining my life or anything like that. I still get through all my work and manage my accounts just fine. \n\nI mean... I guess I have been a little slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. Just taking a bit more time in the mirror, making sure the angle is right. And... well, maybe I'm a little less present during some appointments, just because that thought is in the back of my mind. \n\nAnd, um, I have skipped a few of my wife's art openings recently. I just... I didn't really want to be seen by all those people, or have to pose for photographs, you know? But everyone skips an event now and then, right? It's just a minor thing. I'm still functioning perfectly well overall. That doesn't sound too bad, does it? I just like to be careful about my image.\n\nDoctor: If you were certain that your jaw looked perfectly normal to everyone else, would you still be concerned about its appearance?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, if I knew for absolute certain that everyone else thought it looked totally fine? It's really just about making sure I'm putting my best foot forward with people. \n\nIf I knew they weren't seeing what I'm seeing, then there'd be no reason to stress over it, right? I just want to make sure I'm not making a bad impression.\n\nDoctor: Would your concern about your jaw decrease if you could be certain that others perceive it as normal, even if you personally still see it as uneven?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yeah, I think I pretty much just said that, didn't I? I definitely don't have a whole list of things I'm unhappy with or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others actually notice your jaw or is it just that you're worried about the possibility of them noticing it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, to be completely honest, no one has ever actually said anything to me about it. I guess... it's probably just me worrying about the possibility, like you said. \n\nI definitely don't walk around thinking everyone is staring at me—I don't want you to think I'm paranoid or anything like that! It's really just that little nagging thought in the back of my mind, especially when I'm sitting across the desk from a new client. But since nobody has ever brought it up or stared, I suppose it's mostly just my own internal worry. \n\nThat's a pretty normal distinction to make, right? Just being a little overly self-conscious rather than actually having people point it out? I hope that sounds reasonable. I just try to be self-aware, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be concerned about your jaw if you were the only person who ever saw it?\n\nPatient: Oh, wow, um... that's an interesting way to think about it. I guess if I were literally the only person on earth, or if I lived on a deserted island or something... no, I probably wouldn't worry about it nearly as much. \n\nI mean, I'd still see it in the mirror, obviously, but without the pressure of having to look sharp for clients or... or just wanting to make a good impression in general, it wouldn't feel like such a big deal. \n\nSo much of it is tied to just wanting to present myself well, you know? Being a professional. If nobody was around to see me, I think I'd just let it go. That makes sense, doesn't it? That the social and professional aspect is what really drives the concern? I think most guys in my position would feel the same way if you took the social pressure away.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your concern about your jaw is more about the way it actually looks or about what others might think of you because of it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I guess it's a little bit of both, but... mostly it's about what others might think. I mean, I see it, and I don't love it, but if I knew for a fact that absolutely no one else cared or judged me for it, it wouldn't bother me nearly as much. \n\nIt's just... in my line of work, people are trusting you with their life savings, you know? You want to look like someone who has everything together. So I just worry that if my jaw looks... off, or weak, or whatever, they might subconsciously think I'm not capable or confident. \n\nI'm not saying I'm terrified of people judging me or anything extreme like that! I just want to project competence. That's a pretty normal thing to care about when you're dealing with clients, right? I just try to be mindful of how I'm coming across. I hope that doesn't make me sound too insecure.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your concern about your jaw is causing you significant distress or impairment in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... \"significant distress\" sounds a bit intense. I wouldn't say it's like, ruining my life or anything like that. I still get through all my work and manage my accounts just fine. \n\nI mean... I guess I have been a little slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. Just taking a bit more time in the mirror, making sure the angle is right. I just... I didn't really want to be seen by all those people, or have to pose for photographs, you know? But everyone skips an event now and then, right? It's just a minor thing. I'm still functioning perfectly well overall. That doesn't sound too bad, does it? I just like to be careful about my image.\n\nDoctor: If you were certain that your jaw looked perfectly normal to everyone else, would you still be concerned about its appearance?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, if I knew for absolute certain that everyone else thought it looked totally fine? I guess... I guess I probably wouldn't worry about it. \n\nI mean, if I'm being honest, I'd probably still look in the mirror and think it could be better, you know? But if I was guaranteed that no one else was noticing anything wrong, I think I could let it go. It's really just about making sure I'm putting my best foot forward with people. \n\nIf I knew they weren't seeing what I'm seeing, then there'd be no reason to stress over it, right? I think anyone would feel relieved if they knew people were seeing them in a positive light. That makes sense, doesn't it? I just want to make sure I'm not making a bad impression.\n\nDoctor: Would your concern about your jaw decrease if you could be certain that others perceive it as normal, even if you personally still see it as uneven?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yeah, I think I pretty much just said that, didn't I? I mean, I might still wish it looked a little stronger, but the worry would definitely go way down. \n\nIt really is just about how it comes across to my clients and people I meet. If they aren't bothered by it, then I shouldn't be either, right? I think that's a pretty reasonable way to look at it, don't you? I mean, I definitely think it looks a bit uneven, you know? Like I said, I can see it in the mirror. \n\nBut I suppose... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... well, I feel like we kind of just touched on that, didn't we? I really don't want to sound like a broken record or waste your time. 

But, um, yes, absolutely. If I knew for sure that no one else saw it as a flaw, I wouldn't feel self-conscious at all. Like I said, it's really just about how it looks to my clients. I'm not obsessed with perfection or anything, I just want to make a good impression. I hope that's the right kind of answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to figure this out.

I certainly don't want you to think I'm, like, completely out of touch with reality or anything. \n\nI think a lot of it is just... well, in my line of work, projecting confidence is so important. When you're advising people on their finances, they need to see someone who looks put-together and capable. So I guess I just worry that if my jaw looks weak or uneven, clients might subconsciously judge me for it, or maybe take me less seriously. \n\nSo it's probably a bit of both, but mostly the worry about how it comes across to other people. That makes sense, right? Wanting to make a good, solid impression professionally? I'm sure most people in business worry about that sort of thing... don't they?\n\nDoctor: Is your concern primarily about this specific issue with your jaw, or do you also worry about your overall appearance?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, it's really just the jaw. Just being a little overly self-conscious rather than actually having people point it out? I hope that sounds reasonable. I just try to be self-aware, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be concerned about your jaw if you were the only person who ever saw it?\n\nPatient: Oh, wow, um... that's an interesting way to think about it. I guess if I were literally the only person on earth, or if I lived on a deserted island or something... no, I probably wouldn't worry about it nearly as much. \n\nI mean, I'd still see it in the mirror, obviously, but without the pressure of having to look sharp for clients or... or just wanting to make a good impression in general, it wouldn't feel like such a big deal. \n\nSo much of it is tied to just wanting to present myself well, you know? Being a professional. If nobody was around to see me, I think I'd just let it go. That makes sense, doesn't it? That the social and professional aspect is what really drives the concern? I think most guys in my position would feel the same way if you took the social pressure away.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your concern about your jaw is more about the way it actually looks or about what others might think of you because of it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I guess it's a little bit of both, but... mostly it's about what others might think. I mean, I see it, and I don't love it, but if I knew for a fact that absolutely no one else cared or judged me for it, it wouldn't bother me nearly as much. \n\nIt's just... in my line of work, people are trusting you with their life savings, you know? You want to look like someone who has everything together. I hope that doesn't make me sound too insecure.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your concern about your jaw is causing you significant distress or impairment in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... \"significant distress\" sounds a bit intense. I wouldn't say it's like, ruining my life or anything like that. I still get through all my work and manage my accounts just fine. \n\nI mean... I just try to be self-aware, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be concerned about your jaw if you were the only person who ever saw it?\n\nPatient: Oh, wow, um... that's an interesting way to think about it. I guess if I were literally the only person on earth, or if I lived on a deserted island or something... no, I probably wouldn't worry about it nearly as much. \n\nI mean, I'd still see it in the mirror, obviously, but without the pressure of having to look sharp for clients or... or just wanting to make a good impression in general, it wouldn't feel like such a big deal. \n\nSo much of it is tied to just wanting to present myself well, you know? Being a professional. If nobody was around to see me, I think I'd just let it go. That makes sense, doesn't it? That the social and professional aspect is what really drives the concern? I think most guys in my position would feel the same way if you took the social pressure away.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your concern about your jaw is more about the way it actually looks or about what others might think of you because of it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I guess it's a little bit of both, but... mostly it's about what others might think. I mean, I see it, and I don't love it, but if I knew for a fact that absolutely no one else cared or judged me for it, it wouldn't bother me nearly as much. \n\nIt's just... in my line of work, people are trusting you with their life savings, you know? You want to look like someone who has everything together. So I just worry that if my jaw looks... off, or weak, or whatever, they might subconsciously think I'm not capable or confident. \n\nI'm not saying I'm terrified of people judging me or anything extreme like that! I just want to project competence. That's a pretty normal thing to care about when you're dealing with clients, right? I just try to be mindful of how I'm coming across. I hope that doesn't make me sound too insecure.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your concern about your jaw is causing you significant distress or impairment in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... \"significant distress\" sounds a bit intense. I wouldn't say it's like, ruining my life or anything like that. I still get through all my work and manage my accounts just fine. \n\nI mean... I guess I have been a little slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. Just taking a bit more time in the mirror, making sure the angle is right. And... well, maybe I'm a little less present during some appointments, just because that thought is in the back of my mind. \n\nAnd, um, I have skipped a few of my wife's art openings recently. I just... I didn't really want to be seen by all those people, or have to pose for photographs, you know? But everyone skips an event now and then, right? It's just a minor thing. I'm still functioning perfectly well overall. That doesn't sound too bad, does it? I just like to be careful about my image.\n\nDoctor: If you were certain that your jaw looked perfectly normal to everyone else, would you still be concerned about its appearance?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, if I knew for absolute certain that everyone else thought it looked totally fine? I guess... I guess I probably wouldn't worry about it. \n\nI mean, if I'm being honest, I'd probably still look in the mirror and think it could be better, you know? But if I was guaranteed that no one else was noticing anything wrong, I think I could let it go. It's really just about making sure I'm putting my best foot forward with people. \n\nIf I knew they weren't seeing what I'm seeing, then there'd be no reason to stress over it, right? I think anyone would feel relieved if they knew people were seeing them in a positive light. That makes sense, doesn't it? I just want to make sure I'm not making a bad impression.\n\nDoctor: Would your concern about your jaw decrease if you could be certain that others perceive it as normal, even if you personally still see it as uneven?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yeah, I think I pretty much just said that, didn't I? I just try to be self-aware, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be concerned about your jaw if you were the only person who ever saw it?\n\nPatient: Oh, wow, um... that's an interesting way to think about it. Being a professional. If nobody was around to see me, I think I'd just let it go. That makes sense, doesn't it? That the social and professional aspect is what really drives the concern? I think most guys in my position would feel the same way if you took the social pressure away.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your concern about your jaw is more about the way it actually looks or about what others might think of you because of it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I guess it's a little bit of both, but... mostly it's about what others might think. I mean, I see it, and I don't love it, but if I knew for a fact that absolutely no one else cared or judged me for it, it wouldn't bother me nearly as much. \n\nIt's just... in my line of work, people are trusting you with their life savings, you know? You want to look like someone who has everything together. So I just worry that if my jaw looks... off, or weak, or whatever, they might subconsciously think I'm not capable or confident. \n\nI'm not saying I'm terrified of people judging me or anything extreme like that! I just want to project competence. That's a pretty normal thing to care about when you're dealing with clients, right? I just try to be mindful of how I'm coming across. I hope that doesn't make me sound too insecure.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your concern about your jaw is causing you significant distress or impairment in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... \"significant distress\" sounds a bit intense. I wouldn't say it's like, ruining my life or anything like that. I still get through all my work and manage my accounts just fine. \n\nI mean... I guess I have been a little slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. Just taking a bit more time in the mirror, making sure the angle is right. And... well, maybe I'm a little less present during some appointments, just because that thought is in the back of my mind. \n\nAnd, um, I have skipped a few of my wife's art openings recently. I just... I didn't really want to be seen by all those people, or have to pose for photographs, you know? But everyone skips an event now and then, right? It's just a minor thing. I'm still functioning perfectly well overall. That doesn't sound too bad, does it? I just like to be careful about my image.\n\nDoctor: If you were certain that your jaw looked perfectly normal to everyone else, would you still be concerned about its appearance?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, if I knew for absolute certain that everyone else thought it looked totally fine? I guess... I guess I probably wouldn't worry about it. \n\nI mean, if I'm being honest, I'd probably still look in the mirror and think it could be better, you know? But if I was guaranteed that no one else was noticing anything wrong, I think I could let it go. It's really just about making sure I'm putting my best foot forward with people. \n\nIf I knew they weren't seeing what I'm seeing, then there'd be no reason to stress over it, right? I think anyone would feel relieved if they knew people were seeing them in a positive light. That makes sense, doesn't it? I just want to make sure I'm not making a bad impression.\n\nDoctor: Would your concern about your jaw decrease if you could be certain that others perceive it as normal, even if you personally still see it as uneven?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yeah, I think I pretty much just said that, didn't I? That's pretty normal, right? Wanting to look professional?\n\nAnd I guess... well, I do catch myself looking at other people sometimes. Like, if I'm at an event or even just looking at some of the younger guys at the firm. I'll kind of, um, compare my jaw to theirs. I know I shouldn't, and I really try to focus on what they're saying, but my mind just sort of slips into doing it. I hope that doesn't make me sound too superficial. I'm really just trying to figure out if it's as noticeable as I think it is.\n\nDoctor: How much distress or anxiety would you say this concern about your jaw is causing you?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say it's severe distress or anything like that. I'm definitely not having panic attacks or, you know, completely falling apart over it. It's more just... a mild annoyance, I guess? Like a low-level hum of anxiety in the background. \n\nIt's just that it bothers me when I think about it too much, but I can usually push it aside. It's not like I'm sitting around crying about it. I mean, it's just a jaw, right? It's silly to get too worked up over it. I just... I wish I didn't think about it as much as I do. But it's really not that bad, honestly. I'm sure you see people who are dealing with much, much worse. I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time with something so minor. Does that make sense? I just want to be honest without making it sound like a bigger deal than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these checks or adjustments at specific times or in a specific way?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no, definitely not. I don't have, like, a strict routine or anything like that. It's not like I *have* to do it at a certain time of day or in a specific order. I don't want you to think I'm... you know, doing some kind of strange ritual. \n\nIt's really just when the thought pops up, or right before a meeting like I mentioned. Just a quick glance. I don't have any kind of rigid rules for it. That would probably be a bit much, wouldn't it? I just try to keep it casual... just a normal kind of grooming check, basically. I hope that's what most guys do? I definitely don't have a whole system for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your jaw looks the way you think it does, or is it mostly that you're worried about what others might think of it?\n\nPatient: Well, um... that's a really good question. I mean, I definitely see it when I look in the mirror. It looks... off to me. But, I don't know, maybe I'm just being my own worst critic? I certainly don't want you to think I'm, like, completely out of touch with reality or anything. \n\nI think a lot of it is just... well, in my line of work, projecting confidence is so important. When you're advising people on their finances, they need to see someone who looks put-together and capable. So I guess I just worry that if my jaw looks weak or uneven, clients might subconsciously judge me for it, or maybe take me less seriously. \n\nSo it's probably a bit of both, but mostly the worry about how it comes across to other people. That makes sense, right? Wanting to make a good, solid impression professionally? I'm sure most people in business worry about that sort of thing... don't they?\n\nDoctor: Is your concern primarily about this specific issue with your jaw, or do you also worry about your overall appearance?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, it's really just the jaw. I mean, I try to look presentable, obviously—you know, wear a nice suit, keep my hair neat for the clients. But I'm definitely not sitting around worrying about my weight or... or my nose or anything else. I'm pretty much just a regular guy in that department. \n\nI don't want you to think I'm incredibly vain or, like, obsessed with my looks overall. I hope I'm making sense. I definitely don't have a whole list of things I'm unhappy with or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others actually notice your jaw or is it just that you're worried about the possibility of them noticing it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, to be completely honest, no one has ever actually said anything to me about it. It's really just that little nagging thought in the back of my mind, especially when I'm sitting across the desk from a new client. Just being a little overly self-conscious rather than actually having people point it out? I hope that sounds reasonable. I mean, I see it, and I don't love it, but if I knew for a fact that absolutely no one else cared or judged me for it, it wouldn't bother me nearly as much. \n\nIt's just... in my line of work, people are trusting you with their life savings, you know? My mind just drifts to wondering if they're looking at my jaw. I've also, um... I've skipped a few local art openings recently with my wife. I just really didn't want to be seen, or, you know, end up in the background of someone's photos. I know that sounds a bit foolish, letting something so small get in the way. I'm sorry, I hope I'm not overreacting by bringing this up... is it normal for something like this to be so distracting?\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing anything repeatedly, like checking your appearance or trying to fix your jaw, in response to this concern?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do check it a little bit. I mean, not constantly, but... right before I meet with a client, I might just pop into the bathroom real quick to look in the mirror. And maybe I'll just, you know, glance at my phone camera a couple of times. It's just to make sure the lighting isn't making it look worse than it is. Sometimes I'll adjust my collar a bit, or just try to make sure I'm sitting at an angle where the shadow isn't so obvious. It's just a quick check, really, just to be sure I look presentable. That's pretty normal, right? Wanting to look professional?\n\nAnd I guess... well, I do catch myself looking at other people sometimes. Like, if I'm at an event or even just looking at some of the younger guys at the firm. I'll kind of, um, compare my jaw to theirs. I know I shouldn't, and I really try to focus on what they're saying, but my mind just sort of slips into doing it. I hope that doesn't make me sound too superficial. I'm really just trying to figure out if it's as noticeable as I think it is.\n\nDoctor: How much distress or anxiety would you say this concern about your jaw is causing you?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say it's severe distress or anything like that. I'm definitely not having panic attacks or, you know, completely falling apart over it. It's more just... a mild annoyance, I guess? Like a low-level hum of anxiety in the background. \n\nIt's just that it bothers me when I think about it too much, but I can usually push it aside. It's not like I'm sitting around crying about it. I mean, it's just a jaw, right? It's silly to get too worked up over it. I just... I wish I didn't think about it as much as I do. But it's really not that bad, honestly. I'm sure you see people who are dealing with much, much worse. I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time with something so minor. Does that make sense? I just want to be honest without making it sound like a bigger deal than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these checks or adjustments at specific times or in a specific way?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no, definitely not. I don't have, like, a strict routine or anything like that. It's not like I *have* to do it at a certain time of day or in a specific order. I don't want you to think I'm... you know, doing some kind of strange ritual. \n\nIt's really just when the thought pops up, or right before a meeting like I mentioned. Just a quick glance. I don't have any kind of rigid rules for it. That would probably be a bit much, wouldn't it? I just try to keep it casual... just a normal kind of grooming check, basically. I hope that's what most guys do? I definitely don't have a whole system for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your jaw looks the way you think it does, or is it mostly that you're worried about what others might think of it?\n\nPatient: Well, um... that's a really good question. I mean, I definitely see it when I look in the mirror. It looks... off to me. But, I don't know, maybe I'm just being my own worst critic? I certainly don't want you to think I'm, like, completely out of touch with reality or anything. \n\nI think a lot of it is just... well, in my line of work, projecting confidence is so important. When you're advising people on their finances, they need to see someone who looks put-together and capable. So I guess I just worry that if my jaw looks weak or uneven, clients might subconsciously judge me for it, or maybe take me less seriously. \n\nSo it's probably a bit of both, but mostly the worry about how it comes across to other people. That makes sense, right? Wanting to make a good, solid impression professionally? I'm sure most people in business worry about that sort of thing... don't they?\n\nDoctor: Is your concern primarily about this specific issue with your jaw, or do you also worry about your overall appearance?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, it's really just the jaw. I mean, I try to look presentable, obviously—you know, wear a nice suit, keep my hair neat for the clients. But I'm definitely not sitting around worrying about my weight or... or my nose or anything else. I'm pretty much just a regular guy in that department. \n\nI don't want you to think I'm incredibly vain or, like, obsessed with my looks overall. It's really just this one specific thing that kind of... catches my attention. That's fairly normal, right? To just have one little feature that bugs you, but otherwise you're fine with how you look? I hope I'm making sense. I definitely don't have a whole list of things I'm unhappy with or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others actually notice your jaw or is it just that you're worried about the possibility of them noticing it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, to be completely honest, no one has ever actually said anything to me about it. So I don't have any, like, proof that they're looking at it. I guess... it's probably just me worrying about the possibility, like you said. \n\nI definitely don't walk around thinking everyone is staring at me—I don't want you to think I'm paranoid or anything like that! It's really just that little nagging thought in the back of my mind, especially when I'm sitting across the desk from a new client. But since nobody has ever brought it up or stared, I suppose it's mostly just my own internal worry. \n\nThat's a pretty normal distinction to make, right? Just being a little overly self-conscious rather than actually having people point it out? I hope that sounds reasonable. I just try to be self-aware, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be concerned about your jaw if you were the only person who ever saw it?\n\nPatient: Oh, wow, um... that's an interesting way to think about it. I guess if I were literally the only person on earth, or if I lived on a deserted island or something... no, I probably wouldn't worry about it nearly as much. \n\nI mean, I'd still see it in the mirror, obviously, but without the pressure of having to look sharp for clients or... or just wanting to make a good impression in general, it wouldn't feel like such a big deal. \n\nSo much of it is tied to just wanting to present myself well, you know? Being a professional. If nobody was around to see me, I think I'd just let it go. That makes sense, doesn't it? That the social and professional aspect is what really drives the concern? I think most guys in my position would feel the same way if you took the social pressure away.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your concern about your jaw is more about the way it actually looks or about what others might think of you because of it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I guess it's a little bit of both, but... mostly it's about what others might think. I mean, I see it, and I don't love it, but if I knew for a fact that absolutely no one else cared or judged me for it, it wouldn't bother me nearly as much. \n\nIt's just... in my line of work, people are trusting you with their life savings, you know? You want to look like someone who has everything together. So I just worry that if my jaw looks... off, or weak, or whatever, they might subconsciously think I'm not capable or confident. \n\nI'm not saying I'm terrified of people judging me or anything extreme like that! I just want to project competence. That's a pretty normal thing to care about when you're dealing with clients, right? I just try to be mindful of how I'm coming across. I hope that doesn't make me sound too insecure.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your concern about your jaw is causing you significant distress or impairment in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... \"significant distress\" sounds a bit intense. I wouldn't say it's like, ruining my life or anything like that. I still get through all my work and manage my accounts just fine. \n\nI mean... I guess I have been a little slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. Just taking a bit more time in the mirror, making sure the angle is right. And... well, maybe I'm a little less present during some appointments, just because that thought is in the back of my mind. \n\nAnd, um, I have skipped a few of my wife's art openings recently. I just... I didn't really want to be seen by all those people, or have to pose for photographs, you know? But everyone skips an event now and then, right? It's just a minor thing. I'm still functioning perfectly well overall. That doesn't sound too bad, does it? I just like to be careful about my image.\n\nDoctor: If you were certain that your jaw looked perfectly normal to everyone else, would you still be concerned about its appearance?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, if I knew for absolute certain that everyone else thought it looked totally fine? I guess... I guess I probably wouldn't worry about it. \n\nI mean, if I'm being honest, I'd probably still look in the mirror and think it could be better, you know? But if I was guaranteed that no one else was noticing anything wrong, I think I could let it go. It's really just about making sure I'm putting my best foot forward with people. \n\nIf I knew they weren't seeing what I'm seeing, then there'd be no reason to stress over it, right? I think anyone would feel relieved if they knew people were seeing them in a positive light. That makes sense, doesn't it? I just want to make sure I'm not making a bad impression.\n\nDoctor: Would your concern about your jaw decrease if you could be certain that others perceive it as normal, even if you personally still see it as uneven?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yeah, I think I pretty much just said that, didn't I? I hope I'm being clear enough. \n\nBut yes, if I knew for a fact that everyone else thought my jaw was completely normal... even if I still looked in the mirror and thought it looked uneven, I think I'd be okay with it. I mean, I might still wish it looked a little stronger, but the worry would definitely go way down. \n\nIt really is just about how it comes across to my clients and people I meet. If they aren't bothered by it, then I shouldn't be either, right? I think that's a pretty reasonable way to look at it, don't you? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers here.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your concern about your jaw is something you think is actually wrong with your appearance, or is it more about how you think others might evaluate you as a person because of it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I guess I'm not entirely sure how to separate those two things. I mean, I definitely think it looks a bit uneven, you know? Like I said, I can see it in the mirror. \n\nBut I suppose... I don't know, maybe it's not so much that I think it makes me a bad person or anything. I don't think people are looking at my jaw and deciding I'm incompetent at managing their finances. At least, I really hope they aren't! \n\nI just... I want to look professional and put together. I don't think it's some huge moral failing on my part, or that people are judging my character because of it. It's just a physical thing that I'd like to fix so I don't have to worry about it distracting people. That's a pretty normal way to feel, right? I'm not trying to make a big deal out of it, I just like things to look neat. Does that answer your question? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the need to hide or fix your jaw if you were alone and there was no chance of anyone seeing you?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I really don't think so. If I was completely alone, like, say I was just spending the whole weekend by myself in the house? I probably wouldn't even think about it, to be honest. \n\nI mean, I'm not... I don't just sit around by myself staring in the mirror or anything like that. It's really just about being presentable for work and, you know, being out in public. If there was absolutely zero chance of anyone seeing me, I wouldn't feel the need to hide it at all. I'd just relax. \n\nI think most people probably let their guard down when they're alone, right? You don't have to worry about looking professional if nobody's there. So, yeah, I'd be totally fine. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining this the right way for you, I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel self-conscious about your jaw if you were certain that others did not view it as a flaw, but you personally still saw it as imperfect?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I feel like we kind of just touched on that, didn't we? I really don't want to sound like a broken record or waste your time. \n\nBut, um, yes, absolutely. If I knew for sure that no one else saw it as a flaw, I wouldn't feel self-conscious at all. Like I said, it's really just about how it looks to my clients. If they think it's perfectly fine, then I'm fine with it too. I might still look in the mirror and think, \"Well, that could be a bit straighter,\" but it wouldn't bother me or make me feel anxious about going to work. \n\nI just want to be presentable, you know? I think that's a pretty normal expectation for someone in my line of work. I'm not obsessed with perfection or anything, I just want to make a good impression. I hope that's the right kind of answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to figure this out.\n\nDoctor: Would your concern about your jaw decrease if you could change your profession to one where physical appearance is not important?\n\nPatient: Oh, wow, um... I guess I've never really thought about that. I mean, I've been doing financial advising for a long time, so it's a little hard to picture doing something totally different. \n\nBut... I suppose if I did work somewhere where nobody cared how I looked at all? Like, maybe a job where I just worked from home in my sweatpants? Um, yeah, I imagine the concern would definitely decrease. Since, like I was saying, it's really just about looking sharp and put-together for the clients. \n\nI definitely wouldn't want to actually change careers over it, though! It's not, you know, ruining my life or anything drastic like that. It's really just a... a minor annoyance I'd like to smooth out. I'm not trying to sound dramatic about it. Does that answer the question okay? I'm not entirely sure if I'm doing a good job with these hypotheticals, but I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your concern about your jaw is more about the potential impact it could have on how clients perceive your professionalism, or about the jaw itself appearing imperfect to you?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yeah, definitely the first one.
